+func TestInstallGitHook_SelfHealAfterThirdPartyOverwrite(t *testing.T) {
+	_, hooksDir := initHooksTestRepo(t)
+
+	// Install Entire hooks, then simulate a third-party tool overwriting them
+	if _, err := InstallGitHook(true); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("initial install error: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	thirdPartyContents := make(map[string]string)
+	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
+		content := "#!/bin/sh\n# third_party_tool managed hook\nrun_third_party \"" + hook + "\" \"$@\"\n"
+		thirdPartyContents[hook] = content
+		if err := os.WriteFile(filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook), []byte(content), 0o755); err != nil {
+			t.Fatalf("failed to overwrite %s: %v", hook, err)
+		}
+	}
+
+	// Detection: hooks should be seen as missing
+	if IsGitHookInstalled() {
+		t.Fatal("IsGitHookInstalled() = true after overwrite, want false")
+	}
+
+	// Self-heal reinstall
+	count, err := InstallGitHook(true)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("self-heal error: %v", err)
+	}
+	if count == 0 {
+		t.Error("self-heal should install hooks (count > 0)")
+	}
+	if !IsGitHookInstalled() {
+		t.Error("hooks should be detected after self-heal")
+	}
+
+	// Each hook should have Entire marker, chain to backup, and backup should contain third-party content
+	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
+		data, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook))
+		if err != nil {
+			t.Fatalf("hook %s missing: %v", hook, err)
+		}
+		if !strings.Contains(string(data), entireHookMarker) {
+			t.Errorf("hook %s missing Entire marker", hook)
+		}
+		if !strings.Contains(string(data), chainComment) {
+			t.Errorf("hook %s missing chain call", hook)
+		}
+
+		backupData, err := os.ReadFile(filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook+backupSuffix))
+		if err != nil {
+			t.Fatalf("backup for %s missing: %v", hook, err)
+		}
+		if string(backupData) != thirdPartyContents[hook] {
+			t.Errorf("backup for %s = %q, want third-party content", hook, string(backupData))
+		}
+	}
+}
+
+func TestInstallGitHook_SelfHealAfterHooksDeleted(t *testing.T) {
+	_, hooksDir := initHooksTestRepo(t)
+
+	if _, err := InstallGitHook(true); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("initial install error: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	// Simulate third-party tool deleting all hooks
+	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
+		if err := os.Remove(filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook)); err != nil {
+			t.Fatalf("failed to delete %s: %v", hook, err)
+		}
+	}
+
+	if IsGitHookInstalled() {
+		t.Fatal("IsGitHookInstalled() = true after deletion, want false")
+	}
+
+	count, err := InstallGitHook(true)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("self-heal error: %v", err)
+	}
+	if count != len(gitHookNames) {
+		t.Errorf("self-heal installed %d hooks, want %d", count, len(gitHookNames))
+	}
+	if !IsGitHookInstalled() {
+		t.Error("hooks should be detected after self-heal")
+	}
+
+	// No backups should exist (nothing to back up)
+	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
+		if _, err := os.Stat(filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook+backupSuffix)); !os.IsNotExist(err) {
+			t.Errorf("backup for %s should not exist when hook was deleted", hook)
+		}
+	}
+}
+
+func TestInstallGitHook_SelfHealPreservesOriginalBackup(t *testing.T) {
+	_, hooksDir := initHooksTestRepo(t)
+
+	// User has a custom hook before Entire is ever installed
+	originalContent := "#!/bin/sh\necho 'user original hook'\n"
+	hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, "prepare-commit-msg")
+	if err := os.WriteFile(hookPath, []byte(originalContent), 0o755); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to create custom hook: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	// First install backs up the user's hook
+	if _, err := InstallGitHook(true); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("initial install error: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	// Third-party tool overwrites our hook (but backup still exists)
+	thirdPartyContent := "#!/bin/sh\n# third_party_tool managed\nrun_third_party prepare-commit-msg\n"
+	if err := os.WriteFile(hookPath, []byte(thirdPartyContent), 0o755); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("failed to overwrite: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	// Self-heal
+	if _, err := InstallGitHook(true); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("self-heal error: %v", err)
+	}
+
+	// The ORIGINAL user backup must be preserved, not overwritten with third-party content
+	backupData, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath + backupSuffix)
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("backup should exist: %v", err)
+	}
+	if string(backupData) != originalContent {
+		t.Errorf("original backup overwritten: got %q, want %q", string(backupData), originalContent)
+	}
+}

     1→package strategy
     2→
     3→import (
     4→	"context"
     5→	"errors"
     6→	"fmt"
     7→	"os"
     8→	"os/exec"
     9→	"path/filepath"
    10→	"strings"
    11→
    12→	"github.com/entireio/cli/cmd/entire/cli/settings"
    13→)
    14→
    15→// Hook marker used to identify Entire CLI hooks
    16→const entireHookMarker = "Entire CLI hooks"
    17→
    18→const backupSuffix = ".pre-entire"
    19→const chainComment = "# Chain: run pre-existing hook"
    20→
    21→// gitHookNames are the git hooks managed by Entire CLI
    22→var gitHookNames = []string{"prepare-commit-msg", "commit-msg", "post-commit", "pre-push"}
    23→
    24→// hookSpec defines a git hook's name and content template (without chain call).
    25→type hookSpec struct {
    26→	name    string
    27→	content string
    28→}
    29→
    30→// GetGitDir returns the actual git directory path by delegating to git itself.
    31→// This handles both regular repositories and worktrees, and inherits git's
    32→// security validation for gitdir references.
    33→func GetGitDir() (string, error) {
    34→	return getGitDirInPath(".")
    35→}
    36→
    37→// getGitDirInPath returns the git directory for a repository at the given path.
    38→// It delegates to `git rev-parse --git-dir` to leverage git's own validation.
    39→func getGitDirInPath(dir string) (string, error) {
    40→	ctx := context.Background()
    41→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-dir")
    42→	cmd.Dir = dir
    43→	output, err := cmd.Output()
    44→	if err != nil {
    45→		return "", errors.New("not a git repository")
    46→	}
    47→
    48→	gitDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
    49→
    50→	// git rev-parse --git-dir returns relative paths from the working directory,
    51→	// so we need to make it absolute if it isn't already
    52→	if !filepath.IsAbs(gitDir) {
    53→		gitDir = filepath.Join(dir, gitDir)
    54→	}
    55→
    56→	return filepath.Clean(gitDir), nil
    57→}
    58→
    59→// IsGitHookInstalled checks if all generic Entire CLI hooks are installed.
    60→func IsGitHookInstalled() bool {
    61→	gitDir, err := GetGitDir()
    62→	if err != nil {
    63→		return false
    64→	}
    65→	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
    66→		hookPath := filepath.Join(gitDir, "hooks", hook)
    67→		data, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // Path is constructed from constants
    68→		if err != nil {
    69→			return false
    70→		}
    71→		if !strings.Contains(string(data), entireHookMarker) {
    72→			return false
    73→		}
    74→	}
    75→	return true
    76→}
    77→
    78→// buildHookSpecs returns the hook specifications for all managed hooks.
    79→func buildHookSpecs(cmdPrefix string) []hookSpec {
    80→	return []hookSpec{
    81→		{
    82→			name: "prepare-commit-msg",
    83→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
    84→# %s
    85→%s hooks git prepare-commit-msg "$1" "$2" 2>/dev/null || true
    86→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
    87→		},
    88→		{
    89→			name: "commit-msg",
    90→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
    91→# %s
    92→# Commit-msg hook: strip trailer if no user content (allows aborting empty commits)
    93→%s hooks git commit-msg "$1" || exit 1
    94→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
    95→		},
    96→		{
    97→			name: "post-commit",
    98→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
    99→# %s
   100→# Post-commit hook: condense session data if commit has Entire-Checkpoint trailer
   101→%s hooks git post-commit 2>/dev/null || true
   102→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
   103→		},
   104→		{
   105→			name: "pre-push",
   106→			content: fmt.Sprintf(`#!/bin/sh
   107→# %s
   108→# Pre-push hook: push session logs alongside user's push
   109→# $1 is the remote name (e.g., "origin")
   110→%s hooks git pre-push "$1" || true
   111→`, entireHookMarker, cmdPrefix),
   112→		},
   113→	}
   114→}
   115→
   116→// InstallGitHook installs generic git hooks that delegate to `entire hook` commands.
   117→// These hooks work with any strategy - the strategy is determined at runtime.
   118→// If silent is true, no output is printed (except backup notifications, which always print).
   119→// Returns the number of hooks that were installed (0 if all already up to date).
   120→func InstallGitHook(silent bool) (int, error) {
   121→	gitDir, err := GetGitDir()
   122→	if err != nil {
   123→		return 0, err
   124→	}
   125→
   126→	hooksDir := filepath.Join(gitDir, "hooks")
   127→	if err := os.MkdirAll(hooksDir, 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions
   128→		return 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to create hooks directory: %w", err)
   129→	}
   130→
   131→	// Determine command prefix based on local_dev setting
   132→	var cmdPrefix string
   133→	if isLocalDev() {
   134→		cmdPrefix = "go run ./cmd/entire/main.go"
   135→	} else {
   136→		cmdPrefix = "entire"
   137→	}
   138→
   139→	specs := buildHookSpecs(cmdPrefix)
   140→	installedCount := 0
   141→
   142→	for _, spec := range specs {
   143→		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, spec.name)
   144→		backupPath := hookPath + backupSuffix
   145→		backupExists := fileExists(backupPath)
   146→
   147→		// Back up existing non-Entire hooks
   148→		existing, existingErr := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   149→		if existingErr == nil && !strings.Contains(string(existing), entireHookMarker) {
   150→			if !backupExists {
   151→				if err := os.Rename(hookPath, backupPath); err != nil {
   152→					return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to back up %s: %w", spec.name, err)
   153→				}
   154→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Backed up existing %s to %s%s\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   155→			} else {
   156→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: replacing %s (backup %s%s already exists from a previous install)\n", spec.name, spec.name, backupSuffix)
   157→			}
   158→			backupExists = true
   159→		}
   160→
   161→		// Chain to backup if one exists
   162→		content := spec.content
   163→		if backupExists {
   164→			content = generateChainedContent(spec.content, spec.name)
   165→		}
   166→
   167→		written, err := writeHookFile(hookPath, content)
   168→		if err != nil {
   169→			return installedCount, fmt.Errorf("failed to install %s hook: %w", spec.name, err)
   170→		}
   171→		if written {
   172→			installedCount++
   173→		}
   174→	}
   175→
   176→	if !silent {
   177→		fmt.Println("✓ Installed git hooks (prepare-commit-msg, commit-msg, post-commit, pre-push)")
   178→		fmt.Println("  Hooks delegate to the current strategy at runtime")
   179→	}
   180→
   181→	return installedCount, nil
   182→}
   183→
   184→// writeHookFile writes a hook file if it doesn't exist or has different content.
   185→// Returns true if the file was written, false if it already had the same content.
   186→func writeHookFile(path, content string) (bool, error) {
   187→	// Check if file already exists with same content
   188→	existing, err := os.ReadFile(path) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   189→	if err == nil && string(existing) == content {
   190→		return false, nil // Already up to date
   191→	}
   192→
   193→	// Git hooks must be executable (0o755)
   194→	if err := os.WriteFile(path, []byte(content), 0o755); err != nil { //nolint:gosec // Git hooks require executable permissions
   195→		return false, fmt.Errorf("failed to write hook file %s: %w", path, err)
   196→	}
   197→	return true, nil
   198→}
   199→
   200→// RemoveGitHook removes all Entire CLI git hooks from the repository.
   201→// If a .pre-entire backup exists, it is restored.
   202→// Returns the number of hooks removed.
   203→func RemoveGitHook() (int, error) {
   204→	gitDir, err := GetGitDir()
   205→	if err != nil {
   206→		return 0, err
   207→	}
   208→
   209→	hooksDir := filepath.Join(gitDir, "hooks")
   210→	removed := 0
   211→	var removeErrors []string
   212→
   213→	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
   214→		hookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook)
   215→		backupPath := hookPath + backupSuffix
   216→
   217→		// Remove the hook if it contains our marker
   218→		data, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // path is controlled
   219→		hookIsOurs := err == nil && strings.Contains(string(data), entireHookMarker)
   220→		hookExists := err == nil
   221→
   222→		if hookIsOurs {
   223→			if err := os.Remove(hookPath); err != nil {
   224→				removeErrors = append(removeErrors, fmt.Sprintf("%s: %v", hook, err))
   225→				continue
   226→			}
   227→			removed++
   228→		}
   229→
   230→		// Restore .pre-entire backup if it exists
   231→		if fileExists(backupPath) {
   232→			if hookExists && !hookIsOurs {
   233→				// A non-Entire hook is present — don't overwrite it with the backup
   234→				fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: %s was modified since install; backup %s%s left in place\n", hook, hook, backupSuffix)
   235→			} else {
   236→				if err := os.Rename(backupPath, hookPath); err != nil {
   237→					removeErrors = append(removeErrors, fmt.Sprintf("restore %s%s: %v", hook, backupSuffix, err))
   238→				}
   239→			}
   240→		}
   241→	}
   242→
   243→	if len(removeErrors) > 0 {
   244→		return removed, fmt.Errorf("failed to remove hooks: %s", strings.Join(removeErrors, "; "))
   245→	}
   246→	return removed, nil
   247→}
   248→
   249→// generateChainedContent appends a chain call to the base hook content,
   250→// so the pre-existing hook (backed up to .pre-entire) is called after our hook.
   251→func generateChainedContent(baseContent, hookName string) string {
   252→	return baseContent + fmt.Sprintf(`%s
   253→_entire_hook_dir="$(dirname "$0")"
   254→if [ -x "$_entire_hook_dir/%s%s" ]; then
   255→    "$_entire_hook_dir/%s%s" "$@"
   256→fi
   257→`, chainComment, hookName, backupSuffix, hookName, backupSuffix)
   258→}
   259→
   260→// isLocalDev reads the local_dev setting from .entire/settings.json
   261→// Works correctly from any subdirectory within the repository.
   262→func isLocalDev() bool {
   263→	s, err := settings.Load()
   264→	if err != nil {
   265→		return false
   266→	}
   267→	return s.LocalDev
   268→}

   267→
   268→	// Check for concurrent sessions and append count if any
   269→	strat := GetStrategy()
   270→	if concurrentChecker, ok := strat.(strategy.ConcurrentSessionChecker); ok {
   271→		if count, err := concurrentChecker.CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints(input.SessionID); err == nil && count > 0 {
   272→			message += fmt.Sprintf("\n  %d other active conversation(s) in this workspace will also be included.\n  Use 'entire status' for more information.", count)
   273→		}

   281→	// Fire EventSessionStart for the current session (if state exists).
   282→	// This handles ENDED → IDLE (re-entering a session).
   283→	// TODO(ENT-221): dispatch ActionWarnStaleSession for ACTIVE/ACTIVE_COMMITTED sessions.
   284→	if state, loadErr := strategy.LoadSessionState(input.SessionID); loadErr != nil {
   285→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to load session state on start: %v\n", loadErr)
   286→	} else if state != nil {
   287→		strategy.TransitionAndLog(state, session.EventSessionStart, session.TransitionContext{})
   288→		if saveErr := strategy.SaveSessionState(state); saveErr != nil {
   289→			fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to update session state on start: %v\n", saveErr)
   290→		}
   291→	}
   292→
   293→	return nil
   294→}

   296→// hookResponse represents a JSON response.
   297→// Used to control whether Agent continues processing the prompt.
   298→type hookResponse struct {
   299→	SystemMessage string `json:"systemMessage,omitempty"`
   300→}
   301→
   302→// outputHookResponse outputs a JSON response to stdout
   303→func outputHookResponse(reason string) error {
   304→	resp := hookResponse{
   305→		SystemMessage: reason,
   306→	}
   307→	if err := json.NewEncoder(os.Stdout).Encode(resp); err != nil {
   308→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to encode hook response: %w", err)
   309→	}
   310→	return nil
   311→}

   280→// TestGetGitAuthorFromRepoReturnsDefaults verifies that GetGitAuthorFromRepo
   281→// returns "Unknown" and "unknown@local" when config is missing.
   282→func TestGetGitAuthorFromRepoReturnsDefaults(t *testing.T) {
   283→	t.Parallel()
   284→
   285→	// Create temp directory for test repo
   286→	tmpDir := t.TempDir()
   287→
   288→	// Initialize repo without setting user config
   289→	repo, err := git.PlainInit(tmpDir, false)
   290→	if err != nil {
   291→		t.Fatalf("Failed to init repo: %v", err)
   292→	}
   293→
   294→	// Import strategy package to test GetGitAuthorFromRepo directly
   295→	// Note: We can't import cli package from integration tests due to import cycles,
   296→	// but we can verify the behavior through the CLI commands above.
   297→
   298→	// Verify repo has no user config set
   299→	cfg, err := repo.Config()
   300→	if err != nil {
   301→		t.Fatalf("Failed to get repo config: %v", err)
   302→	}
   303→
   304→	if cfg.User.Name != "" || cfg.User.Email != "" {
   305→		t.Errorf("Expected empty user config, got Name=%q Email=%q", cfg.User.Name, cfg.User.Email)
   306→	}
   307→}
   308→
   309→// removeUserSection removes the [user] section from git config content.
   310→func removeUserSection(config string) string {
   311→	lines := strings.Split(config, "\n")
   312→	var result []string
   313→	inUserSection := false
   314→
   315→	for _, line := range lines {
   316→		trimmed := strings.TrimSpace(line)
   317→		if strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "[user]") {
   318→			inUserSection = true
   319→			continue
   320→		}
   321→		if inUserSection && strings.HasPrefix(trimmed, "[") {
   322→			inUserSection = false
   323→		}
   324→		if !inUserSection {
   325→			result = append(result, line)
   326→		}
   327→	}
   328→
   329→	return strings.Join(result, "\n")
   330→}
   331→

   212→		return 0
   213→	}

   215→	// Delegate to strategy package for the actual count
   216→	return strategy.CountTodos(input.Todos)
   217→}

   219→// logPostTaskHookContext logs the PostToolUse[Task] hook context to the writer
   220→func logPostTaskHookContext(w io.Writer, input *PostTaskHookInput, subagentTranscriptPath string) {
   221→	_, _ = fmt.Fprintln(w, "[entire] PostToolUse[Task] hook invoked")
   222→	_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "  Session ID: %s\n", input.SessionID)
   223→	_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "  Tool Use ID: %s\n", input.ToolUseID)
   224→
   225→	if input.AgentID != "" {
   226→		_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "  Agent ID: %s\n", input.AgentID)
   227→	} else {
   228→		_, _ = fmt.Fprintln(w, "  Agent ID: (none)")
   229→	}
   230→
   231→	_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "  Transcript: %s\n", input.TranscriptPath)
   232→
   233→	if subagentTranscriptPath != "" {
   234→		_, _ = fmt.Fprintf(w, "  Subagent Transcript: %s\n", subagentTranscriptPath)
   235→	} else {
   236→		_, _ = fmt.Fprintln(w, "  Subagent Transcript: (none)")
   237→	}
   238→}
   239→
   240→// handleSessionStartCommon is the shared implementation for session start hooks.
   241→// Used by both Claude Code and Gemini CLI handlers.
   242→func handleSessionStartCommon() error {
   243→	ag, err := GetCurrentHookAgent()
   244→	if err != nil {
   245→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get agent: %w", err)
   246→	}
   247→
   248→	input, err := ag.ParseHookInput(agent.HookSessionStart, os.Stdin)
   249→	if err != nil {
   250→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hook input: %w", err)
   251→	}
   252→
   253→	logCtx := logging.WithAgent(logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "hooks"), ag.Name())
   254→	logging.Info(logCtx, "session-start",
   255→		slog.String("hook", "session-start"),
   256→		slog.String("hook_type", "agent"),
   257→		slog.String("model_session_id", input.SessionID),
   258→		slog.String("transcript_path", input.SessionRef),
   259→	)
   260→
   261→	if input.SessionID == "" {
   262→		return errors.New("no session_id in input")
   263→	}
   264→
   265→	// Build informational message
   266→	message := "\n\nPowered by Entire:\n  This conversation will be linked to your next commit."
   267→
   268→	// Check for concurrent sessions and append count if any
   269→	strat := GetStrategy()
   270→	if concurrentChecker, ok := strat.(strategy.ConcurrentSessionChecker); ok {
   271→		if count, err := concurrentChecker.CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints(input.SessionID); err == nil && count > 0 {
   272→			message += fmt.Sprintf("\n  %d other active conversation(s) in this workspace will also be included.\n  Use 'entire status' for more information.", count)
   273→		}
   274→	}
   275→
   276→	// Output informational message using agent-specific format
   277→	if err := outputHookResponse(message); err != nil {
   278→		return err
   279→	}

Now I have a complete picture. Here's my review:

## PR 284 Review

**Title:** Added self heal for third party overwriting Entire's githooks

**Summary:** The PR adds self-healing logic at session start to detect and reinstall git hooks that may have been overwritten by third-party tools like lefthook.

### Changes

**`cmd/entire/cli/hooks.go`** (+9 lines)
```go
// Self-heal if git hooks were overwritten.
if !strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {
    if n, err := strategy.InstallGitHook(true); err != nil {
        _, _ = fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Warning: failed to reinstall git hooks: %v\n", err)
    } else if n > 0 {
        _, _ = fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "[entire] Reinstalled %d git hook(s) (chained with existing hooks)\n", n)
    }
}
```

**`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/hooks_test.go`** (+130 lines)
Three test cases covering:
1. Third-party tool overwrites hooks → detected and reinstalled with chaining
2. Third-party tool deletes hooks → reinstalled (no backup created)
3. Original user backup is preserved if it already exists

### Issues

1. **Placement in wrong function:** The code is added to `handleSessionStartCommon()` which handles agent session-start hooks (Claude Code, Gemini). But looking at the line numbers from the diff (after line 273), it appears to be placed **after** the `outputHookResponse()` call. This means the self-heal happens after the user message is already output - which is fine, but the order seems intentional to not delay the response.

2. **Missing from user-prompt-submit:** The session-start hook runs once when a session begins. If hooks get overwritten mid-session (between turns), they won't be detected until the next session. This is acknowledged in the PR description as "followup needed."

3. **No unit test for the integration point:** The tests cover `InstallGitHook` and `IsGitHookInstalled` in isolation, but there's no test verifying the new code path in `handleSessionStartCommon()` actually calls them.

---

## Regarding git commit setup checks

**Yes, there is existing code for this** - but it's not a session-start check.

The code in `cmd/entire/cli/git_operations.go:34` (`GetGitAuthor`) handles missing git config by:
1. First trying go-git's repo config
2. Falling back to `git config user.name` / `git config user.email` CLI commands
3. Returning defaults (`"Unknown"`, `"unknown@local"`) if nothing is configured

This is tested in `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/git_author_test.go` which verifies:
- Fallback to git command when go-git can't find config
- Returns defaults when no config exists anywhere
- Works after removing local `[user]` section

The key difference: **git author config is checked lazily when creating commits**, not proactively at session start. The system gracefully degrades to defaults rather than failing.

> AGENT

You're correct. Let me verify the full picture:

**Existing code in `ManualCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()` (manual_commit.go:114-117):**
```go
// Install generic hooks (they delegate to strategy at runtime)
if !IsGitHookInstalled() {
    _, err := InstallGitHook(true)
    return err
}
```

**Called from the stop hook** (`commitWithMetadata` in hooks_claudecode_handlers.go:301):
```go
// Ensure strategy setup is in place (auto-installs git hook, gitignore, etc. if needed)
if err := strat.EnsureSetup(); err != nil {
    fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Warning: failed to ensure strategy setup: %v\n", err)
}
```

So yes, **the manual-commit strategy already self-heals hooks on every turn end** via `EnsureSetup()`.

However, there's one gap: **AutoCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()** does NOT check `IsGitHookInstalled()` - it only ensures gitignore and the metadata branch.

So PR 284 is:
1. **Redundant for manual-commit** - already covered by `EnsureSetup()` on each stop hook
2. **Adds coverage for auto-commit** - which currently lacks this check
3. **Different timing** - session-start vs turn-end

You could point this out to the PR author - for manual-commit strategy the existing `EnsureSetup()` already handles it. The PR would be more targeted if it either:
- Added the check to `AutoCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()` instead (for consistency), or
- Is scoped only to auto-commit scenarios

Would you like me to add a comment on the PR?

> DEVELOPER

For auto commit we only need the push hook, or?

> AGENT

Good point. Let me check what hooks auto-commit actually needs.

    27→
    28→// isNotFoundError checks if an error represents a "not found" condition in go-git.
    29→// This includes entry not found, file not found, directory not found, and object not found.
    30→func isNotFoundError(err error) bool {
    31→	return errors.Is(err, object.ErrEntryNotFound) ||
    32→		errors.Is(err, object.ErrFileNotFound) ||
    33→		errors.Is(err, object.ErrDirectoryNotFound) ||
    34→		errors.Is(err, plumbing.ErrObjectNotFound) ||
    35→		errors.Is(err, plumbing.ErrReferenceNotFound)
    36→}
    37→
    38→// commitOrHead attempts to create a commit. If the commit would be empty (files already
    39→// committed), it returns HEAD hash instead. This handles the case where files were
    40→// modified during a session but already committed by the user before the hook runs.
    41→func commitOrHead(repo *git.Repository, worktree *git.Worktree, msg string, author *object.Signature) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
    42→	commitHash, err := worktree.Commit(msg, &git.CommitOptions{Author: author})
    43→	if errors.Is(err, git.ErrEmptyCommit) {
    44→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "No changes to commit (files already committed)\n")
    45→		head, err := repo.Head()
    46→		if err != nil {
    47→			return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
    48→		}
    49→		return head.Hash(), nil
    50→	}
    51→	if err != nil {
    52→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to commit: %w", err)
    53→	}
    54→	return commitHash, nil
    55→}
    56→
    57→// AutoCommitStrategy implements the auto-commit strategy:
    58→// - Code changes are committed to the active branch (like commit strategy)
    59→// - Session logs are committed to a shadow branch (like manual-commit strategy)
    60→// - Code commits can reference the shadow branch via trailers
    61→type AutoCommitStrategy struct {
    62→	// checkpointStore manages checkpoint data on entire/checkpoints/v1 branch
    63→	checkpointStore *checkpoint.GitStore
    64→	// checkpointStoreOnce ensures thread-safe lazy initialization
    65→	checkpointStoreOnce sync.Once
    66→	// checkpointStoreErr captures any error during initialization
    67→	checkpointStoreErr error
    68→}
    69→
    70→// getCheckpointStore returns the checkpoint store, initializing it lazily if needed.
    71→// Thread-safe via sync.Once.
    72→func (s *AutoCommitStrategy) getCheckpointStore() (*checkpoint.GitStore, error) {
    73→	s.checkpointStoreOnce.Do(func() {
    74→		repo, err := OpenRepository()
    75→		if err != nil {
    76→			s.checkpointStoreErr = fmt.Errorf("failed to open repository: %w", err)
    77→			return
    78→		}
    79→		s.checkpointStore = checkpoint.NewGitStore(repo)
    80→	})

> AGENT

You're right. Looking at the code:

**AutoCommitStrategy only implements `PrePush`** (auto_commit.go:113-119):
```go
// PrePush is called by the git pre-push hook before pushing to a remote.
func (s *AutoCommitStrategy) PrePush(remote string) error {
```

It does **not** implement `PrepareCommitMsg` or `PostCommit` - those are only implemented by ManualCommitStrategy.

**Why auto-commit doesn't need the other hooks:**
- **prepare-commit-msg**: Auto-commit creates commits programmatically via `SaveChanges()` with trailers already added
- **commit-msg**: Same reason - no user-created commits to validate
- **post-commit**: Auto-commit doesn't need to react to user commits (it creates its own via the stop hook)
- **pre-push**: Only one needed - to push `entire/checkpoints/v1` metadata branch alongside user's push

So for auto-commit, hook self-healing is much less important - only pre-push matters, and only for pushing metadata. The PR 284 adds overhead checking/installing 4 hooks when auto-commit only needs 1.

**Summary for PR 284:**
1. **Manual-commit**: Already has self-healing via `EnsureSetup()` on every stop hook - PR is redundant
2. **Auto-commit**: Only uses pre-push, and `EnsureSetup()` doesn't check hooks at all - but also doesn't really need them except pre-push

The PR could be simplified to just add the hook check to `AutoCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()` for consistency, though even that's marginal value since auto-commit only cares about pre-push.

  "new_string": "// EnsureSetup ensures the strategy's required setup is in place.\n// For auto-commit strategy:\n// - Ensure .entire/.gitignore has all required entries\n// - Create orphan entire/checkpoints/v1 branch if it doesn't exist\n// - Install git hooks if missing (self-healing for third-party overwrites)\nfunc (s *AutoCommitStrategy) EnsureSetup() error {\n\tif err := EnsureEntireGitignore(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\trepo, err := OpenRepository()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to open git repository: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Ensure the entire/checkpoints/v1 orphan branch exists\n\tif err := EnsureMetadataBranch(repo); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to ensure metadata branch: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Install generic hooks if missing (they delegate to strategy at runtime)\n\tif !IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t_, err := InstallGitHook(true)\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}"
}

> AGENT

All tests pass. The fix is complete.

**Summary of change:**

Added hook self-healing to `AutoCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()` (auto_commit.go:451-473) to match the existing behavior in `ManualCommitStrategy.EnsureSetup()`:

```go
// Install generic hooks if missing (they delegate to strategy at runtime)
if !IsGitHookInstalled() {
    _, err := InstallGitHook(true)
    return err
}
```

Now both strategies consistently check and reinstall hooks on each stop hook invocation via `EnsureSetup()`. This is a cleaner solution than PR 284's approach of adding a separate check in `handleSessionStartCommon()`.

> DEVELOPER

commit this

> DEVELOPER

Question: For manual strategy we assumed the user commits after a prompt is done, what now is a common pattern also is to say the agent "do this change, and then make a commit" so the commit happens before stop. This means if we do EnsureSetup in stop we might be to late. Is there a good reason not to move EnsureSetup forward to the first hook that is called in a session?

> AGENT

Good point. The hook order is:

1. **session-start** → once per session
2. **user-prompt-submit** → start of each turn (before agent acts)
3. **stop** → end of each turn (after agent finishes)

If the agent commits mid-turn, `prepare-commit-msg` and `post-commit` fire BEFORE `stop`. So if hooks were overwritten, we'd miss them.

Let me check where `EnsureSetup` is currently called and what the user-prompt-submit hook looks like.

    51→

    62→	}, nil
    63→}

    65→// captureInitialState captures the initial state on user prompt submit.

> AGENT

You're right. The current flow is:

```
user-prompt-submit → agent works (may commit mid-turn) → stop
                                    ↑
                            hooks fire here, but EnsureSetup
                            only runs later in stop
```

Moving `EnsureSetup` to `captureInitialState()` (user-prompt-submit handler) makes sense:

1. **Runs at start of each turn** - before any agent actions including mid-turn commits
2. **Cheap if already set up** - `IsGitHookInstalled()` is just a few file reads
3. **More robust** - catches hook overwrites that happened between turns

The only consideration is whether to:
- **Add** it to user-prompt-submit (belt-and-suspenders with stop)
- **Move** it from stop to user-prompt-submit (cleaner, single location)

I'd suggest **move** - since user-prompt-submit runs before stop anyway, having it in stop is redundant. The only edge case would be if someone calls `entire hooks ... stop` directly without a prior user-prompt-submit, but that's not a real usage pattern.

Want me to move `EnsureSetup` from the stop handlers to `captureInitialState()`?

> DEVELOPER

yes

   390→
   391→// handleGeminiBeforeAgent handles the BeforeAgent hook for Gemini CLI.
   392→// This is equivalent to Claude Code's UserPromptSubmit - it fires when the user submits a prompt.
   393→// We capture the initial state here so we can track what files were modified during the session.
   394→// It also checks for concurrent sessions and blocks if another session has uncommitted changes.
   395→func handleGeminiBeforeAgent() error {
   396→	// Always use the Gemini agent for Gemini hooks (don't use GetAgent() which may
   397→	// return Claude based on auto-detection in environments like VSCode)
   398→	ag, err := agent.Get("gemini")
   399→	if err != nil {
   400→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get gemini agent: %w", err)
   401→	}
   402→
   403→	// Parse hook input - BeforeAgent provides user prompt info similar to UserPromptSubmit
   404→	input, err := ag.ParseHookInput(agent.HookUserPromptSubmit, os.Stdin)
   405→	if err != nil {
   406→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to parse hook input: %w", err)
   407→	}
   408→
   409→	logCtx := logging.WithAgent(logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "hooks"), ag.Name())
   410→
   411→	// Log with prompt if available (Gemini provides the user's prompt in BeforeAgent)
   412→	logArgs := []any{
   413→		slog.String("hook", "before-agent"),
   414→		slog.String("hook_type", "agent"),
   415→		slog.String("model_session_id", input.SessionID),
   416→		slog.String("transcript_path", input.SessionRef),
   417→	}
   418→	if input.UserPrompt != "" {
   419→		// Truncate long prompts for logging
   420→		promptPreview := input.UserPrompt
   421→		if len(promptPreview) > 100 {
   422→			promptPreview = promptPreview[:100] + "..."
   423→		}
   424→		logArgs = append(logArgs, slog.String("prompt_preview", promptPreview))
   425→	}
   426→	logging.Info(logCtx, "gemini-before-agent", logArgs...)
   427→
   428→	if input.SessionID == "" {
   429→		return errors.New("no session_id in input")
   430→	}
   431→
   432→	// Capture pre-prompt state with transcript position (Gemini-specific)
   433→	// This captures both untracked files and the current transcript message count
   434→	// so we can calculate token usage for just this prompt/response cycle
   435→	if err := CaptureGeminiPrePromptState(input.SessionID, input.SessionRef); err != nil {
   436→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to capture pre-prompt state: %w", err)
   437→	}
   438→
   439→	// If strategy implements SessionInitializer, call it to initialize session state
   440→	strat := GetStrategy()

> AGENT

The changes look good. Summary:

**Changes made:**

1. **`auto_commit.go`**: Added hook self-healing to `EnsureSetup()` for consistency with manual-commit

2. **`hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`**: 
   - Added `EnsureSetup()` to `captureInitialState()` (user-prompt-submit) 
   - Removed from 3 stop/post-task handlers

3. **`hooks_geminicli_handlers.go`**:
   - Added `EnsureSetup()` to `handleGeminiBeforeAgent()` (before-agent)
   - Removed from `commitGeminiSession()` (after-agent)

**Result:** Hooks are now installed at the start of each turn, before any agent actions including mid-turn commits. Both strategies have consistent self-healing behavior.

Both strategies do three things in `EnsureSetup()`:

1. **`EnsureEntireGitignore()`** - Ensures `.entire/.gitignore` has required entries
2. **`EnsureMetadataBranch(repo)`** - Creates orphan `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch if missing
3. **`IsGitHookInstalled()` / `InstallGitHook()`** - Installs git hooks if missing

> AGENT

**`EnsureSetup()` does three things:**

1. **`EnsureEntireGitignore()`** - Ensures `.entire/.gitignore` contains:
   - `tmp/`
   - `settings.local.json`
   - `metadata/`
   - `logs/`

2. **`EnsureMetadataBranch()`** - Creates orphan `entire/checkpoints/v1` branch with an initial empty commit if it doesn't exist (where condensed session data is stored)

3. **`IsGitHookInstalled()` / `InstallGitHook()`** - Installs the 4 git hooks if missing

All three are idempotent - quick no-ops if already set up. The gitignore check is just string comparisons, the branch check is a single ref lookup, and the hook check reads 4 small files. Running at turn start is fine.

> AGENT

The existing tests cover:

1. **`EnsureSetup()` itself** - called directly in strategy tests (`auto_commit_test.go`)
2. **Integration tests** - many call `SimulateUserPromptSubmit()` which exercises `captureInitialState()`

But there's **no specific test** that verifies:
- `EnsureSetup()` is called during user-prompt-submit (not stop)
- Hooks are installed before mid-turn commits (the scenario we're fixing)
- Self-healing works when hooks are deleted between turns

Want me to add a test that covers the "hooks overwritten, then reinstalled at next turn start" scenario? Something like:

```go
func TestEnsureSetup_CalledOnUserPromptSubmit(t *testing.T) {
    // 1. Setup repo with entire enabled
    // 2. Delete/overwrite git hooks (simulating third-party tool)
    // 3. Call SimulateUserPromptSubmit
    // 4. Verify hooks are reinstalled BEFORE any agent actions
}
```

    15→// Hook marker used to identify Entire CLI hooks
    16→const entireHookMarker = "Entire CLI hooks"
    17→
    18→const backupSuffix = ".pre-entire"
    19→const chainComment = "# Chain: run pre-existing hook"
    20→
    21→// gitHookNames are the git hooks managed by Entire CLI
    22→var gitHookNames = []string{"prepare-commit-msg", "commit-msg", "post-commit", "pre-push"}
    23→
    24→// hookSpec defines a git hook's name and content template (without chain call).
    25→type hookSpec struct {
    26→	name    string
    27→	content string
    28→}
    29→
    30→// GetGitDir returns the actual git directory path by delegating to git itself.
    31→// This handles both regular repositories and worktrees, and inherits git's
    32→// security validation for gitdir references.
    33→func GetGitDir() (string, error) {
    34→	return getGitDirInPath(".")
    35→}
    36→
    37→// getGitDirInPath returns the git directory for a repository at the given path.
    38→// It delegates to `git rev-parse --git-dir` to leverage git's own validation.
    39→func getGitDirInPath(dir string) (string, error) {
    40→	ctx := context.Background()
    41→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "rev-parse", "--git-dir")
    42→	cmd.Dir = dir
    43→	output, err := cmd.Output()
    44→	if err != nil {
    45→		return "", errors.New("not a git repository")
    46→	}
    47→
    48→	gitDir := strings.TrimSpace(string(output))
    49→
    50→	// git rev-parse --git-dir returns relative paths from the working directory,
    51→	// so we need to make it absolute if it isn't already
    52→	if !filepath.IsAbs(gitDir) {
    53→		gitDir = filepath.Join(dir, gitDir)
    54→	}
    55→
    56→	return filepath.Clean(gitDir), nil
    57→}
    58→
    59→// IsGitHookInstalled checks if all generic Entire CLI hooks are installed.
    60→func IsGitHookInstalled() bool {
    61→	gitDir, err := GetGitDir()
    62→	if err != nil {
    63→		return false
    64→	}
    65→	for _, hook := range gitHookNames {
    66→		hookPath := filepath.Join(gitDir, "hooks", hook)
    67→		data, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath) //nolint:gosec // Path is constructed from constants
    68→		if err != nil {
    69→			return false
    70→		}
    71→		if !strings.Contains(string(data), entireHookMarker) {
    72→			return false
    73→		}
    74→	}
    75→	return true
    76→}
    77→
    78→// buildHookSpecs returns the hook specifications for all managed hooks.
    79→func buildHookSpecs(cmdPrefix string) []hookSpec {

    34→// getTestBinary returns the path to the shared test binary.

    43→// TestEnv manages an isolated test environment for integration tests.

    52→// NewTestEnv creates a new isolated test environment.
    53→// It creates temp directories for the git repo and agent project files.
    54→// Note: Does NOT change working directory to allow parallel test execution.
    55→// Note: Does NOT use t.Setenv to allow parallel test execution - CLI commands

    57→func NewTestEnv(t *testing.T) *TestEnv {
    58→	t.Helper()
    59→
    60→	// Resolve symlinks on macOS where /var -> /private/var
    61→	// This ensures the CLI subprocess and test use consistent paths
    62→	repoDir := t.TempDir()
    63→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(repoDir); err == nil {
    64→		repoDir = resolved
    65→	}
    66→	claudeProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    67→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(claudeProjectDir); err == nil {
    68→		claudeProjectDir = resolved
    69→	}
    70→	geminiProjectDir := t.TempDir()
    71→	if resolved, err := filepath.EvalSymlinks(geminiProjectDir); err == nil {
    72→		geminiProjectDir = resolved
    73→	}
    74→
    75→	env := &TestEnv{
    76→		T:                t,
    77→		RepoDir:          repoDir,
    78→		ClaudeProjectDir: claudeProjectDir,
    79→		GeminiProjectDir: geminiProjectDir,
    80→	}
    81→
    82→	// Note: Don't use t.Setenv here - it's incompatible with t.Parallel()
    83→	// CLI commands receive ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR or ENTIRE_TEST_GEMINI_PROJECT_DIR via cmd.Env instead
    84→
    85→	return env
    86→}
    87→
    88→// Cleanup is a no-op retained for backwards compatibility.
    89→//
    90→// Previously this method restored the working directory after NewTestEnv changed it.
    91→// With the refactor to remove os.Chdir from NewTestEnv:
    92→// - Temp directories are now cleaned up automatically by t.TempDir()
    93→// - Working directory is never changed, so no restoration is needed
    94→//
    95→// This method is kept to avoid breaking existing tests that call defer env.Cleanup().
    96→// New tests should not call this method as it serves no purpose.
    97→//
    98→// Deprecated: This method is a no-op and will be removed in a future version.
    99→func (env *TestEnv) Cleanup() {

  "new_string": "func TestSession_CreateTranscript(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\ttranscriptPath := session.CreateTranscript(\"Test prompt\", []FileChange{\n\t\t\t{Path: \"file1.txt\", Content: \"content1\"},\n\t\t\t{Path: \"file2.txt\", Content: \"content2\"},\n\t\t})\n\n\t\t// Verify transcript file exists\n\t\tif _, err := os.Stat(transcriptPath); os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"transcript file should exist\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Verify session ID format\n\t\tif session.ID != \"test-session-1\" {\n\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"session ID = %s, want test-session-1\", session.ID)\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}\n\n// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks verifies that EnsureSetup is called\n// during user-prompt-submit (start of turn) and reinstalls hooks that were overwritten\n// by third-party tools like lefthook. This ensures hooks are in place before any\n// mid-turn commits the agent might make.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\t// Step 1: Verify hooks are installed after InitEntire\n\t\tif !strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after InitEntire\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Overwrite hooks with third-party content (simulating lefthook, husky, etc.)\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\t\thookNames := []string{\"prepare-commit-msg\", \"commit-msg\", \"post-commit\", \"pre-push\"}\n\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tthirdPartyContent := \"#!/bin/sh\\n# Third-party hook manager\\necho 'Running third-party hook'\\n\"\n\t\t\tif err := os.WriteFile(hookPath, []byte(thirdPartyContent), 0o755); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to overwrite hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are no longer Entire hooks\n\t\tif strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected as Entire hooks after overwrite\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Simulate user-prompt-submit (which should call EnsureSetup and reinstall hooks)\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 6: Verify the hooks contain Entire marker AND chain to original hooks\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tcontent, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath)\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"failed to read hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcontentStr := string(content)\n\n\t\t\t// Should have Entire marker\n\t\t\tif !strings.Contains(contentStr, \"Entire CLI hooks\") {\n\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"hook %s should contain 'Entire CLI hooks' marker\", hookName)\n\t\t\t}\n\n\t\t\t// Should chain to pre-existing hook (backup should exist)\n\t\t\tbackupPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName+\".pre-entire\")\n\t\t\tif _, err := os.Stat(backupPath); os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"backup hook %s.pre-entire should exist\", hookName)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}\n\n// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks verifies that EnsureSetup reinstalls\n// hooks that were completely deleted by third-party tools.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\t// Step 1: Verify hooks are installed after InitEntire\n\t\tif !strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after InitEntire\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Delete all hooks (simulating aggressive third-party tool)\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\t\thookNames := []string{\"prepare-commit-msg\", \"commit-msg\", \"post-commit\", \"pre-push\"}\n\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tif err := os.Remove(hookPath); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to delete hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are gone\n\t\tif strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected after deletion\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Simulate user-prompt-submit (which should reinstall hooks)\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}"
}

err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to delete hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are gone\n\t\tif strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected after deletion\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Simulate user-prompt-submit (which should reinstall hooks)\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !strategy.IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}",
  "new_string": "// entireHookMarker is the marker used to identify Entire CLI hooks.\n// Must match the constant in strategy/hooks.go.\nconst entireHookMarker = \"Entire CLI hooks\"\n\n// hookNames are the git hooks managed by Entire CLI.\nvar hookNames = []string{\"prepare-commit-msg\", \"commit-msg\", \"post-commit\", \"pre-push\"}\n\n// hasEntireHooks checks if all Entire CLI hooks are installed in the given repo.\nfunc hasEntireHooks(repoDir string) bool {\n\thooksDir := filepath.Join(repoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\tfor _, hook := range hookNames {\n\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hook)\n\t\tdata, err := os.ReadFile(hookPath)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn false\n\t\t}\n\t\tif !strings.Contains(string(data), entireHookMarker) {\n\t\t\treturn false\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn true\n}\n\n// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks verifies that EnsureSetup is called\n// during user-prompt-submit (start of turn) and reinstalls hooks that were overwritten\n// by third-party tools like lefthook. This ensures hooks are in place before any\n// mid-turn commits the agent might make.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\n\t\t// Step 1: Verify hooks are installed after InitEntire\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after InitEntire\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Overwrite hooks with third-party content (simulating lefthook, husky, etc.)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tthirdPartyContent := \"#!/bin/sh\\n# Third-party hook manager\\necho 'Running third-party hook'\\n\"\n\t\t\tif err := os.WriteFile(hookPath, []byte(thirdPartyContent), 0o755); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to overwrite hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are no longer Entire hooks\n\t\tif hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected as Entire hooks after overwrite\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Simulate user-prompt-submit (which should call EnsureSetup and reinstall hooks)\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 6: Verify the hooks chain to original hooks (backup should exist)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\tbackupPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName+\".pre-entire\")\n\t\t\tif _, err := os.Stat(backupPath); os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"backup hook %s.pre-entire should exist\", hookName)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}\n\n// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks verifies that EnsureSetup reinstalls\n// hooks that were completely deleted by third-party tools.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\n\t\t// Step 1: Verify hooks are installed after InitEntire\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after InitEntire\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Delete all hooks (simulating aggressive third-party tool)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tif err := os.Remove(hookPath); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to delete hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are gone\n\t\tif hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected after deletion\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Simulate user-prompt-submit (which should reinstall hooks)\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}"
}

This ensures hooks are in place before any\n// mid-turn commits the agent might make.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\n\t\t// Step 1: First user-prompt-submit installs hooks via EnsureSetup\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"First SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Verify hooks are now installed\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after first SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Overwrite hooks with third-party content (simulating lefthook, husky, etc.)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tthirdPartyContent := \"#!/bin/sh\\n# Third-party hook manager\\necho 'Running third-party hook'\\n\"\n\t\t\tif err := os.WriteFile(hookPath, []byte(thirdPartyContent), 0o755); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to overwrite hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are no longer Entire hooks\n\t\tif hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected as Entire hooks after overwrite\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Second user-prompt-submit should reinstall hooks\n\t\terr = env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Second SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after second SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 6: Verify the hooks chain to original hooks (backup should exist)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\tbackupPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName+\".pre-entire\")\n\t\t\tif _, err := os.Stat(backupPath); os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"backup hook %s.pre-entire should exist\", hookName)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}\n\n// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks verifies that EnsureSetup reinstalls\n// hooks that were completely deleted by third-party tools.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\n\t\t// Step 1: First user-prompt-submit installs hooks via EnsureSetup\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"First SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Verify hooks are now installed\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after first SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Delete all hooks (simulating aggressive third-party tool)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tif err := os.Remove(hookPath); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to delete hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are gone\n\t\tif hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected after deletion\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Second user-prompt-submit should reinstall hooks\n\t\terr = env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Second SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after second SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit.go",
  "old_string": "// EnsureSetup ensures the strategy's required setup is in place.\n// For auto-commit strategy:\n// - Ensure .entire/.gitignore has all required entries\n// - Create orphan entire/checkpoints/v1 branch if it doesn't exist\nfunc (s *AutoCommitStrategy) EnsureSetup() error {\n\tif err := EnsureEntireGitignore(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\trepo, err := OpenRepository()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to open git repository: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Ensure the entire/checkpoints/v1 orphan branch exists\n\tif err := EnsureMetadataBranch(repo); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to ensure metadata branch: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}",
  "new_string": "// EnsureSetup ensures the strategy's required setup is in place.\n// For auto-commit strategy:\n// - Ensure .entire/.gitignore has all required entries\n// - Create orphan entire/checkpoints/v1 branch if it doesn't exist\n// - Install git hooks if missing (self-healing for third-party overwrites)\nfunc (s *AutoCommitStrategy) EnsureSetup() error {\n\tif err := EnsureEntireGitignore(); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\trepo, err := OpenRepository()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to open git repository: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Ensure the entire/checkpoints/v1 orphan branch exists\n\tif err := EnsureMetadataBranch(repo); err != nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"failed to ensure metadata branch: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\n\t// Install generic hooks if missing (they delegate to strategy at runtime)\n\tif !IsGitHookInstalled() {\n\t\tif _, err := InstallGitHook(true); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}"
}

> AGENT

**Summary of changes:**

1. **`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/auto_commit.go`** (+8 lines)
   - Added hook self-healing to `EnsureSetup()` for consistency with manual-commit strategy

2. **`cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/hooks_test.go`** (+125 lines)
   - Added `hasEntireHooks()` helper to check hooks in a specific repo directory
   - Added `TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks` - verifies hooks are reinstalled when overwritten by third-party tools (lefthook, husky, etc.)
   - Added `TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks` - verifies hooks are reinstalled when deleted

Both tests run for both strategies (`auto-commit` and `manual-commit`) and verify that:
1. First `SimulateUserPromptSubmit` installs hooks via `EnsureSetup()`
2. Hooks can be overwritten/deleted (simulating third-party tools)
3. Second `SimulateUserPromptSubmit` reinstalls the hooks
4. For overwritten hooks, backups are created (`.pre-entire` suffix)

> DEVELOPER

In EnsureSetup(), the error from InstallGitHook(true) is returned without context, while other failures in this method are wrapped (e.g., open repo / ensure metadata branch). Wrapping this error (e.g., "failed to install git hooks") would make failures easier to diagnose and keep error reporting consistent within the function.

This test duplicates the hook marker string and the list of hook names from strategy/hooks.go. Consider exposing these via a small exported helper in the strategy package (e.g., marker getter / managed hook list, or a function that validates hooks for a given git dir) so tests can reference the single source of truth without relying on os.Chdir().

  "old_string": "// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks verifies that EnsureSetup is called\n// during user-prompt-submit (start of turn) and reinstalls hooks that were overwritten\n// by third-party tools like lefthook. This ensures hooks are in place before any\n// mid-turn commits the agent might make.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsOverwrittenHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\n\t\t// Step 1: First user-prompt-submit installs hooks via EnsureSetup\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"First SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Verify hooks are now installed\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after first SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Overwrite hooks with third-party content (simulating lefthook, husky, etc.)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tthirdPartyContent := \"#!/bin/sh\\n# Third-party hook manager\\necho 'Running third-party hook'\\n\"\n\t\t\tif err := os.WriteFile(hookPath, []byte(thirdPartyContent), 0o755); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to overwrite hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are no longer Entire hooks\n\t\tif hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected as Entire hooks after overwrite\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Second user-prompt-submit should reinstall hooks\n\t\terr = env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Second SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after second SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 6: Verify the hooks chain to original hooks (backup should exist)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\tbackupPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName+\".pre-entire\")\n\t\t\tif _, err := os.Stat(backupPath); os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"backup hook %s.pre-entire should exist\", hookName)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}\n\n// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks verifies that EnsureSetup reinstalls\n// hooks that were completely deleted by third-party tools.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\n\t\t// Step 1: First user-prompt-submit installs hooks via EnsureSetup\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"First SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Verify hooks are now installed\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after first SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Delete all hooks (simulating aggressive third-party tool)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tif err := os.Remove(hookPath); err != nil && !os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"failed to delete hook %s: %v\", hookName, err)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 3: Verify hooks are gone\n\t\tif hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should NOT be detected after deletion\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 4: Second user-prompt-submit should reinstall hooks\n\t\terr = env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"Second SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 5: Verify hooks are reinstalled\n\t\tif !hasEntireHooks(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Error(\"hooks should be reinstalled after second SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}",

os.IsNotExist(err) {\n\t\t\t\tt.Errorf(\"backup hook %s.pre-entire should exist\", hookName)\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t})\n}\n\n// TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks verifies that EnsureSetup reinstalls\n// hooks that were completely deleted by third-party tools.\nfunc TestUserPromptSubmit_ReinstallsDeletedHooks(t *testing.T) {\n\tt.Parallel()\n\tRunForAllStrategiesWithRepoEnv(t, func(t *testing.T, env *TestEnv, strategyName string) {\n\t\thooksDir := filepath.Join(env.RepoDir, \".git\", \"hooks\")\n\t\thookNames := strategy.ManagedGitHookNames()\n\n\t\t// Step 1: First user-prompt-submit installs hooks via EnsureSetup\n\t\tsession := env.NewSession()\n\t\terr := env.SimulateUserPromptSubmit(session.ID)\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\tt.Fatalf(\"First SimulateUserPromptSubmit failed: %v\", err)\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Verify hooks are now installed\n\t\tif !strategy.IsGitHookInstalledInDir(env.RepoDir) {\n\t\t\tt.Fatal(\"hooks should be installed after first SimulateUserPromptSubmit\")\n\t\t}\n\n\t\t// Step 2: Delete all hooks (simulating aggressive third-party tool)\n\t\tfor _, hookName := range hookNames {\n\t\t\thookPath := filepath.Join(hooksDir, hookName)\n\t\t\tif err := os.Remove(hookPath); **Summary of changes:**

   107→// PreTaskInput contains the input for PreToolUse[Task] hook.
   108→type PreTaskInput struct {
   109→	SessionID      string
   110→	TranscriptPath string
   111→	ToolUseID      string
   112→	SubagentType   string // Optional: type of subagent (e.g., "dev", "reviewer")
   113→	Description    string // Optional: task description
   114→}
   115→
   116→// SimulatePreTask simulates the PreToolUse[Task] hook.
   117→func (r *HookRunner) SimulatePreTask(sessionID, transcriptPath, toolUseID string) error {
   118→	r.T.Helper()
   119→
   120→	return r.SimulatePreTaskWithInput(PreTaskInput{
   121→		SessionID:      sessionID,
   122→		TranscriptPath: transcriptPath,
   123→		ToolUseID:      toolUseID,
   124→	})
   125→}
   126→
   127→// SimulatePreTaskWithInput simulates the PreToolUse[Task] hook with full input.
   128→func (r *HookRunner) SimulatePreTaskWithInput(input PreTaskInput) error {
   129→	r.T.Helper()
   130→
   131→	hookInput := map[string]interface{}{
   132→		"session_id":      input.SessionID,
   133→		"transcript_path": input.TranscriptPath,
   134→		"tool_use_id":     input.ToolUseID,
   135→		"tool_input": map[string]string{
   136→			"subagent_type": input.SubagentType,
   137→			"description":   input.Description,
   138→		},
   139→	}
   140→
   141→	return r.runHookWithInput("pre-task", hookInput)
   142→}

id: toolu_01Fu95LThxT7W9ct6JjeXnzE
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/resume_test.go",
  "old_string": "func (env *TestEnv) GitMerge(branchName string) {\n\tenv.T.Helper()\n\n\tctx := env.T.Context()\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, \"git\", \"merge\", branchName, \"-m\", \"Merge branch '\"+branchName+\"'\")\n\tcmd.Dir = env.RepoDir\n\n\toutput, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tenv.T.Fatalf(\"failed to merge branch %s: %v\\nOutput: %s\", branchName, err, output)\n\t}\n}",
  "new_string": "func (env *TestEnv) GitMerge(branchName string) {\n\tenv.T.Helper()\n\n\tctx := env.T.Context()\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, \"git\", \"merge\", branchName, \"-m\", \"Merge branch '\"+branchName+\"'\")\n\tcmd.Dir = env.RepoDir\n\t// Set ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR so git hooks can find the test binary\n\tcmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), \"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=\"+env.ClaudeProjectDir)\n\n\toutput, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tenv.T.Fatalf(\"failed to merge branch %s: %v\\nOutput: %s\", branchName, err, output)\n\t}\n}"
}

> AGENT

The issue is that hooks use `go run ./cmd/entire/main.go` when `local_dev: true`, but that path is relative to the working directory. For this test, the merge itself doesn't need to trigger hooks - it's testing resume behavior after a merge. Let me use `--no-verify`:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_011xunTKLx4KcyDnwUSnTK8T
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/soph/Work/entire/devenv/cli/cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/resume_test.go",
  "old_string": "func (env *TestEnv) GitMerge(branchName string) {\n\tenv.T.Helper()\n\n\tctx := env.T.Context()\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, \"git\", \"merge\", branchName, \"-m\", \"Merge branch '\"+branchName+\"'\")\n\tcmd.Dir = env.RepoDir\n\t// Set ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR so git hooks can find the test binary\n\tcmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), \"ENTIRE_TEST_CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR=\"+env.ClaudeProjectDir)\n\n\toutput, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tenv.T.Fatalf(\"failed to merge branch %s: %v\\nOutput: %s\", branchName, err, output)\n\t}\n}",
  "new_string": "func (env *TestEnv) GitMerge(branchName string) {\n\tenv.T.Helper()\n\n\tctx := env.T.Context()\n\t// Use --no-verify to skip hooks - the hooks use local_dev paths that don't work\n\t// from test temp directories. This is fine since we're testing merge behavior,\n\t// not hook execution during merge.\n\tcmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, \"git\", \"merge\", branchName, \"-m\", \"Merge branch '\"+branchName+\"'\", \"--no-verify\")\n\tcmd.Dir = env.RepoDir\n\n\toutput, err := cmd.CombinedOutput()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tenv.T.Fatalf(\"failed to merge branch %s: %v\\nOutput: %s\", branchName, err, output)\n\t}\n}"
}

```diff
diff --git a/cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/resume_test.go b/cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/resume_test.go

> AGENT

You're right - that's a mistake. The comment says "Use go run for hooks in tests" but:

1. Tests actually run hooks via `getTestBinary()` directly (pre-built binary)
2. Git-triggered hooks (like during merge) would try `go run ./cmd/entire/main.go` from the temp dir, which fails

The `local_dev: true` setting shouldn't be there. Tests should use `local_dev: false` so hooks use the `entire` command. But then we'd need to ensure `entire` is available - which it isn't in PATH during tests.

The real question is: do the git hooks installed in test repos ever need to work? Most tests call hooks directly via `getTestBinary()`. The only time actual git hooks fire is during operations like `git merge`, `git commit` (via CLI), etc.

Options:
1. **Remove `local_dev: true`** - hooks would use `entire` which isn't in PATH (would fail)
2. **Keep `--no-verify`** - workaround for operations that trigger git hooks
3. **Don't install hooks in test repos** - since tests call hooks directly anyway

What's your preference? I'd lean toward option 3 - if tests never rely on git-triggered hooks, we shouldn't install them.

> DEVELOPER

but we don't have an option in the cli to not install the hooks, so we would need to add it just for that?

> AGENT

You're right, that would be overkill. The `--no-verify` workaround is simpler and sufficient since:

1. Tests call hooks directly via `getTestBinary()` - that works fine
2. Git-triggered hooks only happen during operations like `GitMerge` 
3. Those operations can use `--no-verify` since we're testing the operation's behavior, not hook behavior

The current fix is reasonable. The `local_dev: true` comment is just misleading - it doesn't actually help tests, but it also doesn't hurt them except in edge cases like `GitMerge` where we work around it.

Want me to at least update the comment to be accurate?

```go
settings := map[string]any{
    "strategy":  strategyName,
    "local_dev": true, // Note: hooks won't work when git triggers them directly; tests call hooks via getTestBinary() instead
}
```
